\int_{B_{1}}\mathcal{A}(\widetilde{u}_{\infty})^{2}|\nabla u_{\infty}|^{2}=0
. Then
\widetilde{u}_{\infty}
would be one-dimensional by
Lemma

2.6
, thus either
\pm 1
or of the form
\phi(a\cdot x+b)
by
Proposition

2.8
, reaching a contradiction exactly as in the previous proof. In Step 2, the proof of Case 1 would follow this exact same reasoning, and Case 2 did not use the subcritical density assumption anyways.
∎

4.2
Large-scale flatness – Proofs of Propositions
3.9
and
3.10

Proofs of Propositions
3.9
and
3.10
.

Step 1.
Density at large scales.

We first show that
K_{*}-\omega(\frac{1}{R})\leq{\bf M}_{R}(\mathbf{z})\leq K_{*}
. The second inequality holds by monotonicity. For the first one, let
K<K_{*}
; we want to see that there is some universal
R_{0}=R_{0}(K)
such that
{\bf M}_{R}(\mathbf{z})>K
if
R\geq R_{0}
. But indeed, by definition of
K_{*}
we know that
K
is subcritical, and in particular we can apply
Theorem

3.2
(with
\varepsilon=1
), from which it follows that if
{\bf M}_{R}(\mathbf{z})\leq K
then
\mathcal{A}_{u}\leq\frac{C}{R}
in
B_{1}(\mathbf{z})
(as long as
R\geq R_{0}(K)
).
On the other hand
\int_{B_{1}(\mathbf{z})}\mathcal{A}_{u}|\nabla u|^{2}\geq\delta_{bad}>0
by assumption, and
|\nabla u|\leq C
is bounded.
This implies that necessarily
{\bf M}_{R}(\mathbf{z})>K
up to making
R_{0}(K)
large enough, as desired.

We are ready to prove the rest of Propositions
3.9
and
3.10
. We assume then for contradiction that there were some
\delta_{1}>0
, and some
R_{i}\to\infty
,
u_{i}
and
\mathbf{z}_{i}
as in the statement, but such that for any given
e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
either

\{|u_{i}|\leq 0.9\}\cap B_{R_{i}}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\not\subset\{|e\cdot(x-\mathbf{z}_{i})|\leq\delta_{1}R_{i}\}

(48)

or

{\bf M}_{\delta_{1}R}(u_{i},y)<K_{*}-\delta_{1}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for some}\hskip 10.00002pty\in\{e\cdot(x-\mathbf{z}_{i})=0\}\cap B_{R_{i}}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\,.

(49)

Step 2.
Rescaling and cone analysis.

Rescaling by
\frac{1}{R_{i}}
, we get solutions
\widetilde{u}_{i}(x):=u_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i}+R_{i}x)
of
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}
-A–C with parameters
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}=\frac{1}{R_{i}}
. By
Theorem

A.3
and
Theorem

A.5
, they will converge—up to subsequence—to a limit stationary stable integral varifold
V=(\Sigma,\theta)
. We will write
\Sigma
instead of
V
and
{\rm supp}\,V
, by slight abuse of notation; our simple arguments will be of very standard nature. Let
\mathbb{M}_{R}(\Sigma,y):=\frac{1}{\omega_{n-1}R^{n-1}}\|\Sigma\|(B_{R}(y))
, and
\mathbb{M}_{R}(\Sigma):=\mathbb{M}_{R}(\Sigma,0)
. Using (
137
) and Step 1 we see that
\mathbb{M}_{R}(\Sigma)=\lim_{i\to\infty}{\bf M}_{RR_{i}}(u_{i})\equiv K_{*}
. Thus, by the monotonicity formula for stationary integral varifolds,
\Sigma
will be a cone, meaning that
\lambda\Sigma=\Sigma
for any
\lambda>0
. We want to see that
\Sigma
is a hyperplane.

All points in
\Sigma
have density at most
K_{*}
, by upper semicontinuity. Define
\mathcal{S}
to be the set of all points of
\Sigma
with density exactly
K_{*}
; since
{\mathbb{M}}_{\infty}(\Sigma)=K_{*}
as well,
\Sigma
is also a cone around any point of
\mathcal{S}
. By standard GMT
15
15

15

Geometric Measure Theory; see for instance
[
Sim18
]
.
arguments,
\mathcal{S}
is actually a linear subspace, the “spine” of
\Sigma
, and
\Sigma+x=\Sigma
for any
x\in\mathcal{S}
. In an appropriate Euclidean frame, we can decompose
\Sigma=C\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
,
1\leq k\leq n
, so that
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
is still a stable minimal hypercone. Moreover, letting
\Theta_{\Sigma}(x):=\lim_{R\to 0}\mathbb{M}_{R}(\Sigma,x)
, we have that

\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}=\mathcal{S}=\{\Theta_{\Sigma}=K_{*}\}=\{\Theta_{\Sigma}\geq K_{*}\}\,.

(50)

The takeaway here is that all points in
C\setminus\{0\}
have density below
K_{*}
. In fact, we have a strict drop, i.e. there exists
16
16

16

Indeed, notice that since
C
is a cone we have
\sup_{x\in(C\setminus\{0\})\times B_{1}^{n-k}}\Theta_{\Sigma}(x)=\sup_{(x^{k},x^{n-k})\in(C\cap\mathbb{S}^{k-1})\times B_{1}^{n-k}}\Theta_{\Sigma}(x^{k},x^{n-k})
. If the strict drop were false, we would find a limit point
x=(x^{k},x^{n-k})\in C\cap\mathbb{S}^{k-1}\times B_{1}^{n-k}
with (by upper semicontinuity of densities)
\Theta_{\Sigma}(x^{k},x^{n-k})=K_{*}
, but (by (
50
)) then
x^{k}=0
, a contradiction with
x^{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
.
some
K=K(\Sigma)<K_{*}
such that
\sup_{x\in(C\setminus\{0\})\times B_{1}^{n-k}}\Theta_{\Sigma}(x)\leq K
.

Let
\delta=\delta(K)>0
be given by
Theorem

3.2
. Let
A=B_{1}^{k}\setminus B_{1/2}^{k}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
By the convergence of the A–C energies to the limit and upper semicontinuity, we easily see that up to making
\delta>0
smaller, for
i
large enough we have
{\bf M}_{\delta}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}(\widetilde{u}_{i},X)\leq K+\delta
for every
X\in(C\cap A)\times B_{1}^{n-k}
. Moreover, up to making
i
larger, we have
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\leq\delta^{2}
. This means that (by
Theorem

3.2
) the sheeting assumptions are satisfied in
B_{\delta^{2}}(X)
for some
C=C(K)
, for every
X\in A\cap\mathbb{S}^{k-1}\times B_{1}
, which gives uniform curvature estimates for
\{u=t\}
for every
|t|\leq 0.9
. Passing to the limit (via Ascoli–Arzelà and Hausdorff convergence), we conclude these level sets converge in
C^{1,\alpha}
to
(C\cap A)\times B_{1}^{n-k}
, and in particular
C\cap A
is
C^{1,\alpha}
. Since
C
is a cone, we deduce that
C\setminus\{0\}
is
C^{1,\alpha}
as well, and therefore smooth by standard bootstrapping results for minimal graphs.

Case 1.
If
3\leq k\leq n
, then Simons’ classification
[
Sim68
]
of hypercones
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
3\leq k\leq 7
, which are smooth and stable outside of the origin gives that
C
(and thus
\Sigma
) is a hyperplane.

Case 2.
If
k=1,2
: If
k=1
,
\Sigma
is trivially a hyperplane. If
k=2
, i.e.
\Sigma=C\times\mathbb{R}^{n-2}
, applying
Theorem

A.6
(given that
\Sigma
is an
\varepsilon
-limit of stable
\varepsilon
-A–C solutions), we conclude that
\Sigma
needs to be a hyperplane as well.

Step 3.
Conclusion.

Choosing an appropriate Euclidean coordinate frame, by the Constancy Theorem (
[
Sim18
, p. 243]
) we have
\Sigma=K_{*}[\{x_{n}=0\}]
, and
K_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
by integrality. In particular, by (
138
) applied to the
\widetilde{u}_{i}
,
\{|\widetilde{u}_{i}|\leq 0.9\}\cap B_{1}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\delta_{1}\}

for
i
large enough, which scaling back gives (
48
) for the
u_{i}
.

We are left with showing that, given
\delta_{1}>0
, we have

{\bf M}_{\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}(\widetilde{u}_{i},y)\geq K_{*}-\delta_{1}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pty\in\{x_{n}=0\}\cap B_{1}

for
i
large enough, since it gives then (
49
) after scaling and thus yields a contradiction.

Now, since
\Sigma=K_{*}[\{x_{n}=0\}]
, obviously
\mathbb{M}_{r}(\Sigma,y)=K_{*}
for any such
y
and
r>0
. Assume there were however
y_{i}\in\{x_{n}=0\}\cap B_{1}
such that
{\bf M}_{\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}(\widetilde{u}_{i},y_{i})<K_{*}-\delta_{1}
instead, and let
y_{\infty}=\lim_{k}y_{i_{k}}
be an accumulation point.

Let
\lambda<1
; by (
137
), we see that
\lim_{i\to\infty}{\bf M}_{\lambda\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i_{k}}}(\widetilde{u}_{i},y_{\infty})=\mathbb{M}_{\lambda\delta_{1}}(\Sigma,y)=K_{*}
. In particular,
{\bf M}_{\lambda\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i_{k}}}(\widetilde{u}_{i_{k}},y_{\infty})\geq K_{*}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{1}
for all
k
large enough.

Now, up to making
k
even larger, we additionally have
B_{\delta_{1}}(y_{i_{k}})\supset B_{\lambda\delta_{1}}(y_{\infty})
. Fixing
\lambda
close enough to
1
(depending on
K_{*}
and
\delta_{1}
), we can then ensure that
{\bf M}_{\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i_{k}}}(\widetilde{u}_{i_{k}},y_{i_{k}})\geq\frac{K_{*}-\delta_{1}}{K_{*}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{1}}{\bf M}_{\lambda\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i_{k}}}(\widetilde{u}_{i_{k}},y_{\infty})
, just by direct comparison. Combining the above we reach
{\bf M}_{\delta_{1}}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i_{k}}}(\widetilde{u}_{i_{k}},y_{i_{k}})\geq K_{*}-\delta_{1}
, a contradiction.
∎

4.3
Tangential stability and bad balls – Proofs of Propositions
3.14
and
3.15

Proof of
Proposition

3.14
.

We will show that, for every
\eta\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}\eta^{2}\,|\nabla u|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left(1-\left(e\cdot\frac{\nabla u}{|\nabla u|}\right)^{2}\right)|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}=\int|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}\,,

which plugging in a standard linear cutoff shows
Proposition

3.14
.

We test stability with
\xi=|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|\eta
, getting

\displaystyle-\int W^{\prime\prime}(u)(|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|\eta)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int|\nabla(|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|\eta)|^{2}=\int|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}|\nabla\eta|^{2}+|\nabla|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u||^{2}\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2})\cdot\nabla\eta^{2}\,.

(51)

On the other hand, differentiating (
5
) in a direction
\tau\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
and multiplying by the directional derivative
u_{\tau}
gives that
u_{\tau}\Delta u_{\tau}=W^{\prime\prime}(u)u_{\tau}^{2}
, which adding over an orthonormal basis
\{\tau_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n-1}
of
\{\tau\cdot e=0\}
and multiplying by
\eta^{2}
shows

\sum_{j}u_{\tau_{j}}\Delta u_{\tau_{j}}\eta^{2}=W^{\prime\prime}(u)|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}\eta^{2}\,.

Integrating by parts, we obtain that

\displaystyle-\int W^{\prime\prime}(u)|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}\eta^{2}=\int\sum_{j}|\nabla u_{\tau_{j}}|^{2}\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int\sum_{j}\nabla u_{\tau_{j}}^{2}\cdot\nabla\eta^{2}=\int\sum_{j}|\nabla u_{\tau_{j}}|^{2}\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int\nabla(|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2})\cdot\nabla\eta^{2}\,.

Combining this with (
51
), we find that

\displaystyle\int\Big(\sum_{j}|\nabla u_{\tau_{j}}|^{2}-|\nabla|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u||^{2}\Big)\eta^{2}\leq\int|\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}|\nabla\eta|^{2}\,,

which since
\sum_{j}|\nabla u_{\tau_{j}}|^{2}=\sum_{j}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\partial_{i}u_{\tau_{j}})^{2}=|D\nabla^{e^{\prime}}u|^{2}
shows the desired result.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.15
.

Assume the proposition is false for contradiction. Then, we find appropriate
e_{k},u_{k}
such that
\int_{B_{1}(x_{k})}\mathcal{A}^{2}(u_{k})|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}\geq\delta_{bad}
but
\int_{B_{1}(x_{k})}\mathcal{A}_{e_{k}}^{\prime 2}(u_{k})|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{k}
. Letting
\widetilde{u}_{k}(x):=u_{k}(x-x_{k})
, up to a subsequence we obtain a stable solution
\widetilde{u}_{\infty}=\lim_{k}\widetilde{u}_{k}
and some
e_{\infty}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
such that
\int_{B_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{2}(\widetilde{u}_{\infty})|\nabla u_{\infty}|^{2}\geq\delta_{bad}
but
\mathcal{A}_{e_{\infty}}^{\prime}(\widetilde{u}_{\infty})\equiv 0
in
B_{1}
. Then, by
Proposition

4.1
below, we deduce that
\widetilde{u}_{\infty}
is two-dimensional. By the 2D stable De Giorgi conjecture (
Theorem

2.9
) then
\widetilde{u}_{\infty}
is either
\pm 1
or of the form
\phi(a\cdot x+b)
, but then
\mathcal{A}(\widetilde{u}_{\infty})\equiv 0
, and we reach a contradiction.
∎

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be a solution to A–C, and assume that
\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime}\equiv 0
for some
e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
in some open set
\Omega
. Then
u
is two-dimensional in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, i.e.
u=v(a_{1}\cdot x,a_{2}\cdot x)
for some orthogonal directions
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
and
v:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

Assume withot loss of generality
e=\boldsymbol{e}_{n}
. Assuming that
\nabla^{\prime}u\neq 0
, we can compute

\displaystyle D\frac{\nabla^{\prime}u}{|\nabla^{\prime}u|}=\frac{D\nabla^{\prime}u-\nabla|\nabla^{\prime}u|\otimes\frac{\nabla^{\prime}u}{|\nabla^{\prime}u|}}{|\nabla^{\prime}u|}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{thus}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}=\left\|D\frac{\nabla^{\prime}u}{|\nabla^{\prime}u|}\right\|^{2}\,.

The same argument as in
Lemma

2.6
then gives the result.
∎

4.4
Height and tilt excesses

The first result is a Caccioppoli-type inequality for the level sets of
u
(rather than
u
itself).

Lemma 4.2

(
Caccioppoli
)
.

Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be an A–C solution, and let
\eta\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Then, there is
C=C(n)
such that

\displaystyle\int|\nabla^{e_{n}^{\prime}}u|^{2}\eta^{2}\leq 4\int|x_{n}|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}|\nabla\eta|^{2}+C\int W(u)|x_{n}\eta\eta_{n}|\,.

Proof.

Obtained as in
[
Wan17
, Remark 4.7]
; we give the proof in
Appendix

C
for convenience of the reader.
∎

Recall the cylinder notation
\mathcal{C}_{r}:=B_{r}^{\prime}\times[-r,r]\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
. The following well-known lemma asserts that most of the energy is concentrated around the intermediate transition layers.

Lemma 4.3

(
Exponential decay).

Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be an A–C solution, and assume that
\{|u|\leq 0.85\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{4R}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\delta R\}
, where
\delta\in(0,1)
and
R\geq 1
. Then, there are dimensional constants
C
and
c_{0}>0
such that

\sup_{\mathcal{C}_{2R}\cap\{|x_{n}|\geq 2\delta R\}}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]\leq Ce^{-c_{0}\delta R}\,.

(52)

Proof.

Standard; a proof is given in
Appendix

C
for convenience of the reader.
∎

We now relate
L^{\infty}
-flatness (“height”) and the
L^{2}
-height excess.

Lemma 4.4

(
Height controls height excess
)
.

Given
\lambda\in(0,1)
, there exist
c_{0}>0
and
C
depending on
\lambda
such that the following holds. Assume that
\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{4R}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\delta R\}
for some
\delta>0
and
R\geq 1
. Then,

{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{n})\leq C\max\{\delta^{2},R^{-2(1-\lambda)}\}{\bf M}_{R}+Ce^{-c_{0}R^{\lambda}}.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\delta}=\max\{\delta,R^{-(1-\lambda)}\}
. If
\widetilde{\delta}\geq 1
, the inequality is trivial. Otherwise, letting
P=\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]
, using
Lemma

4.3
we can estimate

\displaystyle{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(u,e)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{R}}|x\cdot e|^{2}P=\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{R}\cap\{|x\cdot e|\leq 2\widetilde{\delta}R\}}|x\cdot e|^{2}P+\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{R}\cap\{|x\cdot e|>2\widetilde{\delta}R\}}|x\cdot e|^{2}P

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}(2\widetilde{\delta}R)^{2}\int_{B_{R}\cap\{|x\cdot e|\leq 2\widetilde{\delta}R\}}P+\frac{C}{R^{n-1}}e^{-c_{0}\widetilde{\delta}R}\leq C\widetilde{\delta}^{2}{\bf M}_{R}+Ce^{-c_{0}R^{\lambda}}\,.

∎

Lemma 4.5

(
Height excess controls height).

Let
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
. Then, there is
\delta_{2}>0
depending on
\delta_{1},n
such that if
{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{n})\leq\delta_{2}^{2}
, then, for all
R\geq 1
,

\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap B_{R/2}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\max\{\delta_{1},\frac{1}{R}\}R\}\,.

More generally, given additionally
c_{0}>0
, up to making
\delta_{2}>0
smaller we have that

\{x:{\bf M}_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)\geq c_{0}\}\cap B_{R/2}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\delta_{1}R\}\,.

Proof.

Step 1.
Density of intermediate layers.

Let
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}:=\max\{\delta_{1},\frac{1}{R}\}
.
We first see that
\{|u(x)|\leq 0.9\}\subset\{{\bf M}_{\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{1}}{2}R}(x)\geq c_{0}\}
, for some
c_{0}>0
depending on
n
.

Indeed, since
\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(n)
and
W(s)>0
for
s\neq\pm 1
, there are
c>0
and
r_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
depending on
n
such that
W(u)\geq c>0
in
B_{r_{0}}(x)
. Therefore,
{\bf M}_{r_{0}}(x)=\frac{1}{r_{0}^{n-1}}\int_{B_{r_{0}}(x)}\frac{1}{\sigma_{n-1}}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]\geq c_{0}
. On the other hand, since
\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{1}}{2}R\geq\frac{1}{2}>r_{0}
,
Lemma

2.3
gives that
{\bf M}_{\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{1}}{2}R}(x)\geq c_{0}
as well.

Step 2.
Conclusion.

By Step 1, it suffices to prove the second part of the result. If by contradiction there is
x\in B_{R/2}\setminus\{|x_{n}|\leq\delta_{1}R\}
with
M_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)\geq c_{0}
, then (since
B_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)\subset\{|x_{n}|\geq\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R\}\cap B_{R}
) we have that

\displaystyle{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{n})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)}|y_{n}|^{2}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]\,dy\geq\left(\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{R^{n-1}}\int_{B_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]\,dy

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}\right)^{n+1}{\bf M}_{\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}R}(x)\geq c_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}\right)^{n+1}\,,

and then if
{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{n})\leq\delta_{2}^{2}
with
\delta_{2}
small enough we reach a contradiction.
∎

We can finally give:

Proposition 4.6

(
Height excess controls tilt excess
)
.

Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be an A–C solution, with
{\bf M}_{\infty}\leq C_{0}
. Then, there are
C
,
R_{0}
and
c>0
, depending on
C_{0}
and
n
, such that if
R\geq R_{0}
, then

\displaystyle{\bf T}_{R}^{2}(e_{n})\leq C{\bf H}_{2R}^{2}(e_{n})+e^{-cR}\,.

Proof.

We will show that if
r\geq R_{0}
then

{\bf T}_{r}^{2}(e_{n})\leq C{\bf H}_{16r}^{2}(e_{n})+e^{-cr}\,,

(53)

which up to a finite covering argument shows the desired result as well (with some different
C,c,R_{0}
).

Let
\xi\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
be a standard cutoff satisfying
\chi_{[-1,1]}\leq\xi\leq\chi_{[-4/3,4/3]}
, so that
\xi\equiv 1
in
[-1,1]
. Plugging
\eta(x^{\prime},x_{n})=\xi(\frac{|x^{\prime}|}{r})\xi(\frac{x_{n}}{r})
into
Lemma

4.2
, since
\chi_{B_{r}}\leq\eta\leq\chi_{B_{2r}}
and
\eta_{n}\equiv 0
in
\{|x_{n}|\leq r\}
we find

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|\nabla^{e_{n}^{\prime}}u|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{2r}}|x_{n}|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+C\int_{B_{2r}\cap\{|x_{n}|\geq r\}}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]|x_{n}\eta\eta_{n}|\,.

We consider two different cases:

If
\{|u|\leq 0.85\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{4r}\subset\{|x_{n}|\leq\frac{r}{2}\}
, then applying
Lemma

4.3
to the last term we obtain (
53
).

Otherwise,
Lemma

4.5
shows that
{\bf H}_{16r}^{2}(e_{n})\geq c>0
as long as
R\geq R_{0}
is large enough. Since
{\bf T}_{r}^{2}(e_{n})\leq C{\bf M}_{r}\leq C{\bf M}_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded, we conclude (
53
) as well.
∎

We end this section with a general comparison result. Morally, it says that if the level sets can be approximated well by two hyperplanes
x+e_{x}^{\perp}
and
y+e_{y}^{\perp}
, then they are both close in direction and “height”.

Lemma 4.7

(
Coefficient comparison).

Given
C_{0}\geq 1
, there exist
C
and
R_{0}
depending on
C_{0}
,
n
such that the following holds.

Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be an A–C solution. Then, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
\frac{1}{C_{0}}\leq{\bf M}_{R}(x)\leq C_{0}
and
R\geq\max\{\frac{1}{2}{\rm dist}(x,y),R_{0}\}
, for all
e_{x},e_{y}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
we have:

\displaystyle\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|\leq C[{\bf H}_{6R}(e_{x},x)+{\bf H}_{6R}(e_{y},y)]+Ce^{-cR}

(54)

and

\displaystyle|e_{x}\cdot(x-y)|\leq C[{\bf H}_{6R}(e_{x},x)+{\bf H}_{6R}(e_{y},y)]+Ce^{-cR}.

(55)

Proof.

Step 1.
Proof of (
54
).

Combining (
13
) and the lower density assumption, we see that
\frac{1}{R^{n-1}}\int_{B_{R}(x)}|\nabla u|^{2}\geq c
as long as
R\geq R_{0}
, so that in particular

\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{R^{n-1}}\int_{B_{R}(x)}\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}\,.

(56)

By the triangle inequality we can compute

\displaystyle\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}\big|e_{x}|\nabla u|-\sigma\nabla u\big|^{2}+2\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}\left|e_{y}|\nabla u|-\sigma\nabla u|\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\left[\big(2|\nabla u|^{2}-2|\nabla u\cdot e_{x}||\nabla u|\big)+\big(2|\nabla u|^{2}-2|\nabla u\cdot e_{y}||\nabla u|\big)\right]\,,

thus by Cauchy–Schwarz we have

\displaystyle\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\big[(|\nabla u|^{2}-|\nabla u\cdot e_{x}|^{2})+(|\nabla u|^{2}-|\nabla u\cdot e_{y}|^{2})\big]=4\big[|\nabla^{e_{x}^{\prime}}u|^{2}+|\nabla^{e_{y}^{\prime}}u|^{2}\big]\,.

Combining this with (
56
) and using that
B_{R}(x)\subset B_{3R}(y)
, we find that

\displaystyle\min_{\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}}|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\int_{B_{3R}(x)}|\nabla^{e_{x}^{\prime}}u|^{2}+\int_{B_{3R}(y)}|\nabla^{e_{y}^{\prime}}u|^{2}\Big]=C\left[{\bf T}_{3R}^{2}(e_{x},x)+{\bf T}_{3R}^{2}(e_{y},y)\right]\,.

Using
Proposition

4.6
we obtain (
54
).

Step 2.
Proof of (
55
).

Recall that

{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{x},x)=\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{R}(x)}|e_{x}\cdot(X-x)|^{2}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]\,dX\,,

and likewise for
{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{y},y)
. On the other hand, we can bound

|e_{x}\cdot(x-y)|\leq|e_{x}\cdot(X-x)|+|e_{x}-\sigma e_{y}||X|+|e_{y}\cdot(X-y)|\,.

Integrating, and using (
54
) and
{\bf M}_{R}(x)\leq C_{0}
to bound the second term on the right, we readily find (
55
).
∎

4.5
Wang–Wei in a flat setting

We rewrite
Theorem

2.14
in a flat, large-scale setting. This is the version we will use in the rest of the article:

Theorem 4.8

(
[
WW19a
]
in a flat setting
)
.

Let
n\leq 10
. Let
u:B_{R}\to(-1,1)
be a stable A–C solution, and assume the sheeting assumptions hold in
B_{R}
for some
C_{1}
. Let
\delta_{1}>0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
. Then, there exist
C_{2}
depending on
C_{1},\theta
, as well as
R_{0}
depending on
\delta_{1},C_{1},\theta
such that the following hold.

Assume additionally that

\varnothing\neq\{u=0\}\cap B_{R}\subset\{|e_{n}\cdot x|\leq\delta_{2}R\}

for some
\delta_{2}\in(0,1/16)
and
R\geq R_{0}
. Then, there is some
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

•

There are
C^{\infty}
graphs
g_{i}:B_{\frac{3}{5}R}^{\prime}\to[-R/8,R/8]
,
g_{1}<...<g_{N}
, such that
\{u=0\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\frac{3}{5}R}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}
.

•

The estimates (
15
)–(
17
) hold for the
g_{i}
.

Proof.

Immediate from
Lemma

2.13
and
Theorem

2.14
, by making
\delta_{2}>0
sufficiently small.
∎

Moreover, we collect some additional properties.

Lemma 4.9

(
Additional properties).

Let
\delta_{1}>0
. In the setting of
Theorem

4.8
, up to making
\delta_{2}>0
smaller and
R_{0}
larger depending also on
\delta_{1}
, the following additionally hold:

•

The graphs satisfy
\|\nabla g_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\delta_{1}
.

•

There holds
\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\subset\{|e_{n}\cdot x|\leq\delta_{2}R+C_{2}\}
in
\mathcal{C}_{R/2}
.

•

We have
|{\bf M}_{R/2}(u)-N|\leq\delta_{1}N
.

For the proof, we first need:

Lemma 4.10

(
1D approximation).

Let
u:B_{R}\to(-1,1)
be a stable A–C solution, and assume the sheeting assumptions hold in
B_{R}
for some
C_{1}
. Given
b_{1}\in(0,1)
and
\delta>0
, there exists
R_{0}=R_{0}(C_{1},b_{1},\delta,n)
such that if
R\geq R_{0}
, then

\inf_{a\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},\,b\in\mathbb{R}}\|u-\phi(a\cdot x+b)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\frac{1}{\delta}}(x_{0}))}\leq\delta\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for any}\hskip 10.00002ptx_{0}\in\{|u|\leq 1-b_{1}\}\cap B_{R/2}\,.

In other words, making
R
large enough then
u
is close to a
1D
solution around any intermediate point.

Proof of
Lemma

4.10
.

Notice that, by exponential decay (
Lemma

4.3
), there is
C=C(n,b_{1})
such that
\{|u|\leq 1-b_{1}\}\cap B_{R/2}\subset\{x:{\rm dist}(x,\{|u|\leq 0.85\})\leq C\}
for
R_{0}
large enough. It easily follows that it suffices to show the Lemma just with
1-b_{1}=0.85
.

Observe now that, for any
x_{0}\in\{|u|\leq 0.85\}\cap B_{R/2}
, there is
r_{0}=r_{0}(n)>0
such that—up to making
R_{0}
larger—we have
B_{r_{0}}(x_{0})\subset\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap B_{R}
. In particular, the bounds in (
14
) are satisfied at all such points. We conclude by arguing exactly as in
[
WW19a
, Lemma 2.1]
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.9
.

The first bullet follows directly by interpolation, up to making
\delta_{2}>0
small.

For the second one, observe that if
R_{0}
and
C_{2}
are large enough,
Lemma

4.10
ensures that
u
changes sign in
B_{C_{2}}(x)
for any
x\in\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{R/2}
. In particular

{\rm dist}(\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{R/2},\cup_{i=1}^{N}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\frac{3}{4}R})={\rm dist}(\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{R/2},\{u=0\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\frac{3}{4}R})\leq C_{2}\,,

(57)

which gives the second bullet.

For the third one, define
v_{i}(x)=[\textnormal{sign}\,(u)]\phi({\rm dist}(x,{\rm graph}\,g_{i}))
. By the first bullet,
|\nabla g_{i}|\leq\delta_{1}
as long as
R_{0}
is large enough and
\delta_{2}
is small enough. Up to updating these values, this easily shows that
|{\bf M}_{R/2}(v_{i})-1|\leq\delta_{1}
, since
|{\bf M}_{\infty}(\phi)|=1
and
g_{i}
is almost flat.

On the other hand, given
\delta>0
, set
\Omega_{i}:=\{{\rm dist}(x,{\rm graph}\,g_{i})\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\}\cap B_{R/2}
. Thanks to (
57
) and
Lemma

4.3
, we have exponential decay away from
\cup_{i}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}
, thus up to making
\delta
small enough we easily obtain that
\mathcal{E}(u,B_{R/2}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{N}\Omega_{i})\leq\delta_{1}N
. Moreover, using
Lemma

4.10
, it is easy to see that
|u-v_{i}|\leq\delta_{1}
in
\Omega_{i}
as long as
R_{0}
is large enough.

Adding up these facts, the third bullet readily follows.
∎

5
Setting up the contradiction

5.1
General setting

We assume that
n\leq 7
in this section; we will restrict to
n=4
from
Section

6
on.

From now on, we fix a critical solution
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to(-1,1)
, which has density at infinity
{\bf M}_{\infty}=K_{*}<\infty
. In particular,
u
is not 1D.

We will use
C,C_{i},R_{0}
(resp.
c,c_{i},r_{0}
) to denote large (resp. small) positive universal constants—unless otherwise stated—and which may change from line to line.

Lemma 5.1

(
Excess goes to
0
).

Let
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Then,
{\bf H}_{R}(\mathbf{z})\leq\widetilde{\omega}(R^{-1})
, where
\widetilde{\omega}
is a dimensional modulus of continuity.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3.9
, there is
e_{\mathbf{z},R}
such that

\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})\subset\{x:|e_{\mathbf{z},R}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z})|\leq\omega(R^{-1})R\}.

But then, applying
Lemma

4.4
(appropriately translated and rotated) with, say,
\lambda=1/2
, we deduce that
{\bf H}_{\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}R}(e_{\mathbf{z},R},\mathbf{z})=\widetilde{\omega}(R^{-1})
, for some new modulus of continuity
\widetilde{\omega}
.

Thus
{\bf H}_{\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}R}(\mathbf{z})=\widetilde{\omega}(R^{-1})
as well, and considering
4\sqrt{2}R
instead of
R
(and conveniently updating
\widetilde{\omega}
) gives the result.
∎

Proposition 5.2

(
Tangential stability and bad ball count).

Let
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
. If
R\geq R_{0}
, then

R^{3-n}|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z})\cap B_{\frac{3R}{4}}(\mathbf{z})|\leq C{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for any}\hskip 10.00002pte\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},

(58)

thus

R^{3-n}|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z})\cap B_{\frac{3R}{4}}(\mathbf{z})|\leq C{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\,.

(59)

Remark 5.3
.

In particular, for
n=4
we see that

{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\geq\frac{c}{R}\,.

(60)

Proof.

Step 1.
Bad ball count.

We first show that

R^{3-n}|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{3R}{2}})|\leq C{\bf K}_{2R}^{2}(e).

(61)

By
Proposition

3.15
, given any bad center
\mathbf{z}
and any
e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
we know that
\int_{B_{1}(\mathbf{z})}\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}|\nabla u|^{2}\geq\delta_{bad}^{\prime}
. Consider the collection of balls
\{B_{1}(\mathbf{z})\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{3R}{2}}}
for such
\mathbf{z}
, and pass to a disjoint Vitali subcollection
\{B(\mathbf{z}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
, so that
B_{1}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{3R}{2}})\subset\bigcup_{i}B_{5}(\mathbf{z}_{i})
. Since the
B_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})
are disjoint, we can bound

\displaystyle|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{3R}{2}})|\leq 5^{n}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|B_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})|\leq\frac{5^{n}}{\delta_{bad}^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{B_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}|\nabla u|^{2}\leq\frac{5^{n}}{\delta_{bad}^{\prime}}\int_{B_{2R}}\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}|\nabla u|^{2}\,,

where in the last inequality we used that
B_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\subset B_{1+\frac{3R}{2}}\subset B_{2R}
as long as
R\geq 2
. Multiplying by
R^{3-n}
we conclude (
61
).

Step 2.
Comparison of
{\bf T}
and
{\bf H}
.

Recentering (
61
) and combining this with
Proposition

3.14
and
Proposition

4.6
, we see that

R^{3-n}|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z})\cap B_{\frac{3R}{4}}(\mathbf{z})|\leq C{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf T}_{2R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})+e^{-cR}

(62)

for
R\geq R_{0}
large enough.
Since
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
we obviously have
|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z})\cap B_{\frac{3R}{4}}(\mathbf{z})|>c
, and then (
62
) shows in particular that
{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\geq\frac{c}{R^{n-3}}
. Thus,

{\bf T}_{2R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\,.

(63)

We see then that

R^{3-n}|B_{1}(\mathcal{Z})\cap B_{\frac{3R}{4}}(\mathbf{z})|\leq C{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq{\bf T}_{2R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e,\mathbf{z})\,,

(64)

which gives (
58
); taking infima among
e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
, we obtain (
59
).
∎

5.2
Selection of center and scale – Proof of Lemmas
3.17
and
3.18

We first give:

Proof of
Lemma

3.17
.

Given
R\geq R_{0}
, set

F(R):=\sup_{\\
z\in\mathcal{Z}}\frac{{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})}{{\bf K}_{R}^{2(1+\alpha)}(\mathbf{z})},

which corresponds to penalising the stability inequality (
58
) by introducing an
\alpha>0
exponent. The lower bound for
{\bf K}
in (
59
), together with the fact that
{\bf H}
is uniformly bounded (since
{\bf M}
is), shows that
F(R)\leq CR^{(n-3)(1+\alpha)}
. Moreover, tangential stability (i.e. (
59
)) gives that

F(R)=\sup_{z\in Z}\frac{{\bf H}_{4R}^{2(1+\alpha)}(\mathbf{z})}{{\bf K}_{R}^{2(1+\alpha)}(\mathbf{z})}\frac{1}{{\bf H}_{4R}^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{z})}\geq c\sup_{z\in Z}\frac{1}{{\bf H}_{4R}^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{z})}.

Lemma

5.1
then shows that
\lim_{R\to\infty}F(R)=\infty
; defining
\widetilde{F}(R)=\sup_{r\in[R_{0},R]}F(r)
, which is finite and nondecreasing, naturally
\lim_{R\to\infty}\widetilde{F}(R)=\infty
as well.

By definition, we can find
r\in[R_{0},R]
with
\frac{2}{3}\widetilde{F}(R)\leq F(r)\leq\widetilde{F}(R)
, thus trivially
\frac{2}{3}\widetilde{F}(r)\leq F(r)\leq\widetilde{F}(r)
as well. It is then clear that we can find a sequence
R_{k}\to\infty
and associated
\mathbf{z}_{k}\in\mathcal{Z}
with

\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{F}(R_{k})\leq\frac{{\bf H}_{4R_{k}}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k})}{{\bf K}_{R_{k}}^{2(1+\alpha)}(\mathbf{z}_{k})}\leq\widetilde{F}(R_{k})\,,

and setting
\epsilon_{k}:={\bf H}_{4R_{k}}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k})
, which tends to zero by
Lemma

5.1
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Recall the definition of
N(\theta,B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))
, the size of the bad set at resolution
\theta R
, in
Definition

3.16
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
,
R>0
and
\theta\in(0,1]
. Then, we can find disjoint bad balls
\{B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{Q}
such that
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{Q}\subset\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})
, and

cN(\theta,B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))\leq Q\leq CN(\theta,B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\bigcup_{i=1}^{Q}B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\subset B_{\theta R}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{Q}B_{5\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\,.

(65)

Proof.

The collection of balls
B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}})
with
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})
covers
B_{\theta R}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))=\cup_{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})}B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}})
by definition. We can then pass to a
5
-Vitali disjoint subcover, i.e. a finite disjoint subcollection
\{B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{Q}
with the property that the
\{B_{5\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{Q}
still cover
B_{\theta R}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))
. The result is then immediate.
∎

We now give:

Proof of
Lemma

3.18
.

Let

\theta_{k}:=\inf\left\{\theta\geq\frac{R_{0}}{R_{k}}\mbox{ : exists }\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}\mbox{ with }B_{\theta R_{k}}(\mathbf{z})\subset B_{R_{k}}(\mathbf{z}_{k}),{\bf K}_{\theta R_{k}}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\leq\theta^{\beta}{\bf K}_{R_{k}}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\right\}\,.

(66)

Combining (
59
) (with
R=R_{0}
) and (
28
), for
k
large enough we see that
0<\frac{10R_{0}}{R_{k}}\leq\theta_{k}\leq 1
. By definition of infimum, we can choose
\widetilde{\theta}_{k}\in[\theta_{k},\min\{2\theta_{k},1\}]
and
{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}\in\mathcal{Z}
such that, letting
{\widetilde{R}_{k}}:=\widetilde{\theta}_{k}R_{k}
,

B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\subset B_{R_{k}}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\leq\widetilde{\theta}_{k}^{\beta}{\bf K}_{R_{k}}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k}).

(67)

Combining (
67
) with (
28
), in particular we see that

{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\leq 2\left(\frac{{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})}{{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})}\right)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for any}\hskip 10.00002ptB_{R}(\mathbf{z})\subset B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}),

(68)

so that the second inequality in (
30
) follows by using the trivial comparison
{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z})\leq({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{n-3}{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
.
Applying (
68
) with
\mathbf{z}={\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}
and
R={\widetilde{R}_{k}}
, we deduce that
{\bf H}_{4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\leq 2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon_{k}^{2}\to 0
, so that moreover
{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\to\infty
by (
59
).

It remains to see (
31
). Consider
\theta=t\widetilde{\theta}_{k}
with
t\in(\frac{R_{0}}{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}},\frac{1}{2})
, and assume that
N_{\theta}\geq C\theta^{-(n-3+\beta)}
for some
C
large for contradiction; by
Lemma

5.4
, we find
Q\geq\theta^{-(n-3+\beta)}
disjoint bad balls with
\bigcup_{i=1}^{Q}B_{\theta R}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\subset B_{\theta R}(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z}))
. Then, by the disjoint property, we can bound

\sum_{i=1}^{Q}{\bf K}_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\leq\inf_{e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\frac{1}{(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-3}}\int_{B_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}|\nabla u|^{2}\leq\inf_{e\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\frac{1}{(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-3}}\int_{B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})}\mathcal{A}_{e}^{\prime 2}|\nabla u|^{2}=\theta^{-(n-3)}{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\,;

(69)

in particular, since
Q\geq\theta^{-(n-3+\beta)}
there is at least one
j\in\{1,...,Q\}
such that
{\bf K}_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j})\leq\theta^{\beta}{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
,
which contradicts the definition of
\widetilde{\theta}_{k}
and
\theta_{k}
.
∎

6
Graphical decomposition

We restrict to
n=4
in the remainder of the article.

6.1
Graphical direction

We first show that, thanks to (
30
), a single direction dominates at all scales of interest. Recall that
\gamma:=\frac{1}{4}
.

Lemma 6.1

(
Graphical direction).

Let
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\gamma}{8}]=(0,\frac{1}{32}]
. For any given
k
, let us choose a Euclidean coordinate frame
17
17

17

This frame always exists thanks to the second bullet in
Lemma

3.18
.
such that
{\bf H}_{4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}(e_{n},{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\leq 2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
. Then,

{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\to\infty\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\sup_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}),\,R\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]}{\bf H}_{R}^{2}(e_{n},\mathbf{z})\to 0\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{as}\hskip 10.00002ptk\to\infty\,.

(70)

Proof.

Let
{\hat{R}_{k}}:={\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
.

Step 1.
Growth of the smallest scale
{\hat{R}_{k}}
.

Combining
Lemma

3.18
and (
60
) we have that
2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\geq{\bf H}_{4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\geq\frac{c}{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}
, or
{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\geq c{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-2}
. Thus,
{\hat{R}_{k}}\geq c{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-\gamma/2}\xrightarrow{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\to 0}\infty
.

Step 2.
Coefficient comparison.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
R_{l}:=2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\geq\frac{{\hat{R}_{k}}}{2}
. By Step 1, we have that
R_{l}\gg R_{0}
for
k
large enough, so that we can apply (
30
). Hence, given
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
for every
i=1,...,l
there exists some vector
v_{i}
such that

{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(v_{i},\mathbf{z})\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{i})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}=C2^{(1+\alpha)i}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}.

(71)

Observe that we can take
v_{1}=e_{n}
in (
71
), since
{\bf H}_{4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}(e_{n},{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\leq 2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
.

Claim.
We have the bound
{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(e_{n},\mathbf{z})\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{i})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
, for every
i=1,...,l
.

Assuming the claim the second property in (
70
) follows, thanks to the bound

({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{l})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-(2-\gamma/2)(1+\alpha)}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}=C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\gamma/2-2\alpha+\gamma\alpha/2}\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\gamma/4}\xrightarrow{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\to 0}0\,.

To show the claim, observe that (
71
) gives that
{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(v_{i},\mathbf{z})+{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(v_{i+1},\mathbf{z})\leq C2^{(1+\alpha)i}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
.
Applying
Lemma

4.7
, and adding over
i
the corresponding geometric sum, we find that

|v_{j}-v_{i}|^{2}\leq C2^{(1+\alpha)i}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}=C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{i})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for any}\hskip 10.00002pt1\leq j<i\leq l.

Taking
j=1
(recall that
v_{1}=e_{n}
) we find that
|e_{n}-v_{i}|^{2}\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{i})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
, which easily shows that

{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(e_{n},\mathbf{z})\leq C{\bf H}_{4R_{i}}^{2}(v_{i},\mathbf{z})+C|e_{k}-v_{i}|^{2}\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{i})^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}

as desired.
∎

We have only used the second inequality of (
30
), which is a “worst case bound” that holds for every
\mathbf{z}
. For later use, we note the following “stronger” result :

Lemma 6.2

(
Sum of height excesses).

Assume that
R\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]
and
k
is large enough. Then, given
\{\mathbf{z}_{j}\}_{j\in J}\subset\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
with
\{B_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\}_{j\in J}
pairwise disjoint, there exist
v_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
such that

|v_{j}-e_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{j\in J}{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})\leq C\left(\frac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{R}\right)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}.

(72)

Proof.

We will use
18
18

18

To show this: Dividing by
(\sum_{j\in J}a_{j})^{1+\alpha}
, we can assume
\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}=1
, thus in particular
a_{j}\leq 1
for every
j\in J
. But then
a_{j}^{1+\alpha}\leq a_{j}
, so that
\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}^{1+\alpha}\leq\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}=1=(\sum_{j\in J}a_{j})^{1+\alpha}

as desired.
: Given positive numbers
\{a_{j}\}_{j\in J}
and some
\alpha>0
, we have
\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}^{1+\alpha}\leq(\sum_{j\in J}a_{j})^{1+\alpha}
.

By this fact and (
30
), we can estimate

\sum_{j\in J}{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\leq 2\sum_{j\in J}\left(\frac{{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}{{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})}\right)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\leq 2\left(\sum_{j\in J}\frac{{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}{{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})}\right)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}.

(73)

Since the
\{B_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\}_{j\in J}
are pairwise disjoint, arguing as in (
69
) we can bound

\sum_{j\in J}{\bf K}_{R}^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\leq({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{l})^{n-3}{\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})=({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R_{l}){\bf K}_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\,,

so that together with (
73
) and the definition of
{\bf H}
we find
v_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
with

\sum_{j\in J}{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\,.

(74)

By
Lemma

6.1
, since
R\geq{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
we know that
{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(e_{n},\mathbf{z}_{j})=o_{k}(1)
; likewise, (
74
) shows that
{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})=o_{k}(1)
. Applying
Lemma

4.7
, up to possibly changing the sign of the
v_{j}
then
|v_{j}-e_{n}|=o_{k}(1)
as well.
∎

6.2
Decomposing
\{u=0\}
into
K_{*}
layers – Proof of
Proposition

3.19

We can finally show that the level sets of our solution decompose into exactly
K_{*}
graphs away from the bad set.

Proof of
Proposition

3.19
.

Let
{\hat{R}_{k}}:={\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
.

Observe first that, combining
Proposition

3.9
with (
70
) and
Lemma

4.7
, we immediately deduce:

Let
\delta>0
. Then, for
k
large enough, for any
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
and
R\in[{\hat{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]
we have

\{u=0\}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})\subset\{|e_{n}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z})|\leq o_{k}(1)R\}

(75)

and

K_{*}-{\bf M}_{\delta R}(y)\leq o_{k}(1)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pty\in\{e_{n}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z})=0\}\cap B_{R}(\mathbf{z})\,.

(76)

Given
\bar{x}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}
, let
\mathbf{z}(\bar{x}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}/2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
be such that
\rho:={\rm dist}\,(\bar{x}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})=|\bar{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{z}^{\prime}(\bar{x})|
, and put
\bar{x}=(\bar{x}^{\prime},(\mathbf{z}(\bar{x}^{\prime}))_{n})
.
B_{\rho}(\bar{x})
is obviously a good ball, i.e.
B_{\rho}(\bar{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}=\emptyset
. For
k
large enough, the sheeting assumptions are then satisfied in
B_{3\rho/4}(\bar{x})
thanks to
Theorem

3.4
, and moreover (
75
)–(
76
) give that

\{u=0\}\cap B_{\rho}(\bar{x})\subset\{|e_{n}\cdot(x-\bar{x})|\leq o_{k}(1)\rho\}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002ptK_{*}-{\bf M}_{\delta\rho}(\bar{x})\leq o_{k}(1)\,.

(77)

Set
\mathcal{C}_{\rho/2}(\bar{x})=B_{\rho/2}^{\prime}(\bar{x}^{\prime})\times[\bar{x}_{n}-\rho/2,\bar{x}_{n}+\rho/2]
. For
k
large enough, by
Theorem

4.8
and
Lemma

4.9
we see that there are exactly
K_{*}
smooth graphs
g_{i}:B_{\rho/2}^{\prime}(\bar{x}^{\prime})\to[-\rho/8,\rho/8]
,
g_{1}<...<g_{K_{*}}
, such that
\{u=0\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\rho/2}(\bar{x})=\bigcup_{j=1}^{K_{*}}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}
and
|\nabla g_{i}|\leq o_{k}(1)
.

Vertically, these graphs actually cover all of the zero level set: Indeed, given
y\in\{u=0\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
, with
y^{\prime}\in B_{\rho/2}^{\prime}(\bar{x}^{\prime})
, assume for contradiction that
y\notin\bigcup_{j=1}^{K_{*}}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}
, so that
y\notin\mathcal{C}_{\rho/2}(\bar{x})
. This means that
|y_{n}-\bar{x}_{n}|\geq\rho/2
, thus letting
R=2|y_{n}-\bar{x}_{n}|
we see that

y\in\{u=0\}\cap B_{R}(\bar{x})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{but}\hskip 10.00002pt|e_{n}\cdot(y-\bar{x})|=|y_{n}-\bar{x}_{n}|\geq\frac{R}{2}\,,

(78)

which contradicts (
77
) for
k
large enough.

Summing up our argument up to now, given any point in
\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}
, we have obtained a local (horizontally) decomposition of the intermediate level sets as exactly (vertically)
K_{*}
graphs. In particular, given any
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}
, in case their associated decompositions have an overlap then they obviously need to match. Taking the union over
\bar{x}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}
of these graphs, we have shown:

For
k
large enough there are
K_{*}
smooth graphs
g_{i}:\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}\to\mathbb{R}
,
g_{1}<...<g_{K_{*}}
, such that

\{u=0\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\cap(\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}\times\mathbb{R})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{K_{*}}{\rm graph}\,g_{i}\,.

Finally, we already saw that
|\nabla g_{i}|\leq o_{k}(1)
, which together with
Theorem

4.8
(i.e. (
16
)) gives (
35
).
∎

We record moreover the following byproduct of the proof (which follows directly from (
75
)):

Lemma 6.3

(
Large scale flatness).

We have

|g_{i}(x^{\prime})-\mathbf{z}_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1)|x^{\prime}-\mathbf{z}^{\prime}|
in
\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}
,
for every
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
and
i\in\{1,...,K_{*}\}
.

7
Improvement of excess for the graphs – Proof of
Proposition

3.20

The goal of this section is to prove
Proposition

3.20
. It will be a consequence of the following:

Proposition 7.1
.

Fix
\chi\in(0,\frac{1}{20}]
,
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{40}]
and
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{40}]
. There exist a constant
C
and smooth extensions
\widetilde{g}_{i}:B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}
, with
\widetilde{g}_{i}\equiv g_{i}
in
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}
such that the following holds for every
k
large enough:
For any given
\bar{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
, there is an affine function
\ell:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\frac{1}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\chi}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\fint_{B_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\chi}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})}\big|\widetilde{g}_{i}(x^{\prime})-\ell\big|\,dx\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+\chi/3}\hskip 20.00003pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pti\in\{1,...,K_{*}\}\,,

(79)

In particular, (
39
) follows.

7.1
A Whitney-type extension

We construct new Whitney-type extensions which capture (
30
). We need:

Definition 7.2

(
Projected bad set and distance).

In what follows, we set
N_{\theta}:=N\big(\theta,B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\big)
. Moreover, for
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we set
d(x^{\prime}):={\rm dist}\big(x^{\prime},[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime}\big)
and

\displaystyle N_{\theta}^{\prime}:=(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{-3}\Big|B_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}([\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime})\Big|=(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{-3}\Big|{\textstyle\bigcup}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime}}B_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})\Big|\,.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\theta\in(0,1]
. We have
N_{\theta}^{\prime}\leq CN_{\theta}
, so that

|\{d\leq\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})|\leq C(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}N_{\theta}\,.

(80)

Proof.

Since
|\{x^{\prime}:d(x^{\prime})\leq\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})|\leq(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}N_{\theta}^{\prime}
by definition, it suffices to show
N_{\theta}^{\prime}\leq CN_{\theta}
. But indeed, projecting the simple result in
Lemma

5.4
, we find that we can cover
B_{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}([\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime})
with at most
CN(\theta,B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}))=CN_{\theta}
projected bad balls of radius
5\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
, which gives
N_{\theta}^{\prime}\leq CN_{\theta}
.
∎

The main result of this section is:

Proposition 7.4

(
Extension).

There exist smooth functions
\widetilde{g}_{i}:B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}
such that the following hold:

(i)

\widetilde{g}_{i}\equiv g_{i}
in
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}=B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus B_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}([\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime})
.

(ii)

\fint_{B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})}|\widetilde{g}_{i}-({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
.

(iii)

We have
|\nabla\widetilde{g}_{i}|\leq o_{k}(1)
in
B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})
.

(iv)

The mean curvature of
\widetilde{g}_{i}
has the following bound in
L^{1}
, in the region
B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}
where
\widetilde{g}_{i}
and
g_{i}
differ:

\int_{B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}+C\sqrt{N_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+(2-\gamma)(3-\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\,.

(81)

Preliminaries.
Before giving the proof we introduce some definitions and a preparatory lemma. Set

C_{0}:=\lceil{\tfrac{1}{2}\log_{2}(n-1)}\rceil+3=4\hskip 20.00003pt(n=4)\hskip 20.00003pt\mbox{and}\hskip 20.00003ptl_{\star}:=\lceil{-\log_{2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}\rceil+3.

(82)

Dyadic cubes.
For
l\geq C_{0}
, consider the grid of
dyadic cubes
of the form

Q=2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}([0,1)^{n-1}+y^{\prime}),\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{where }y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}\,.

(83)

We say that
Q
has
vertex
at
2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}y^{\prime}
. We define the
triple cube

TQ
by
TQ=2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}([-1,2)^{n-1}+y^{\prime})
.

We denote
\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}:=\{Q\mbox{ of the form \eqref{form_of_Q}}:TQ\cap B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset\}
. We define the
predecessor
of
Q
, denoted
PQ
, as the cube in
\boldsymbol{Q}^{l-1}
which contains
Q
.

Note:
By the choice of
C_{0}
, since
l\geq C_{0}
, if
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
then
TQ\subset B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})
. Furthermore, the Euclidean ball of radius equal to the diameter of
TQ
centered at any point of
TQ
is still contained in
B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})
.

Cutoffs and partition of unity.
Fix a smooth cutoff
\eta_{\circ}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((-1/2,3/2)^{n-1})
such that
\eta_{\circ}\geq 1
in
[0,1]^{n-1}
. We define

\widetilde{\eta}_{\circ}(x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{\circ}(x^{\prime})}{\sum_{\zeta^{\prime}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{n-1}}\eta_{\circ}(x^{\prime}+\zeta^{\prime})}\quad&\mbox{ if }x^{\prime}\in[-1,2]^{n-1}\,,\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise}\,,\end{cases}\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{so that}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}}\widetilde{\eta}_{\circ}(\,\cdot\,+\zeta^{\prime})\equiv 1\,.

We can get a rescaled dyadic version in the obvious way: For
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
with vertex
2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}y^{\prime}
, we put

\eta_{Q}(x^{\prime}):=\widetilde{\eta}_{\circ}\left(\frac{x^{\prime}}{2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}-y^{\prime}\right)\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{so that}\hskip 20.00003pt\sum_{\widetilde{Q}\subset TQ}\eta_{\widetilde{Q}}\equiv 1\mbox{ in }Q\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}}\eta_{Q}\equiv 1\mbox{ in }B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\,.

We emphasise that
\eta_{Q}
is supported in the triple cube
TQ
. Moreover, we obtain
19
19

19

Standard by scaling. The constant depends on
\widetilde{\eta}_{\circ}
, which is fixed depending on the dimension.

|\eta_{Q}|+2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|\nabla\eta_{Q}|+(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}|D^{2}\eta_{Q}|\leq C(n)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ for all }Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}.

(84)

Bad dyadic cubes.
Recall that
2^{-4}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}\leq 2^{-l_{\star}}\leq 2^{-3}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}
. Let us define

X:=\bigcup\big\{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}\ :\ TQ\cap[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime}\neq\varnothing\big\}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002ptTX:=\bigcup\big\{TQ\ :\ Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}},\ Q\subset X\big\}.

In particular, we have the inclusions
20
20

20

For the last inclusion, notice that any point in
TX
must be at most at distance

3\sqrt{n-1}\,2^{-l_{*}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\leq 3\sqrt{n-1}\,2^{-3}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}<{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
from some point in
\big[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\big]^{\prime}
. We have used that
3\sqrt{n-1}\,2^{-3}<1
for
n=4
.

B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\cap B_{2^{-4}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\left(\big[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\big]^{\prime}\right)\subset TX\subset B_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\left(\big[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\big]^{\prime}\right).

(85)

We also consider a larger resolution version: For
C_{0}\leq l\leq l_{*}
we define
\mathcal{X}^{l}:=\left\{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}\,:\,TQ\cap TX\neq\varnothing\right\}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
C_{0}\leq l\leq l_{\star}
. To each
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
, we can assign an affine map
\ell_{Q}:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\to\mathbb{R}
with

R_{k}^{-1}|\ell_{Q}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})-({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}|+|\nabla\ell_{Q}|\leq o_{k}(1)

(86)

such that the following hold. Put

h_{Q}^{2}:=\frac{1}{(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-1}}\int_{TQ\times\big(({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}-\tfrac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}+\tfrac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\big)}|x_{n}-\ell_{Q}(x^{\prime})|^{2}\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]dx^{\prime}dx_{n}\,;

(87)

assuming
TQ\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}\neq\emptyset
then

\frac{1}{(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-1}}\int_{TQ\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}}|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}(x^{\prime})|^{2}dx^{\prime}\leq Ch_{Q}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for all }1\leq i\leq K_{*}\,.

(88)

We then have the bound

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}}\left(\frac{h_{Q}}{2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\right)^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\right)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}.

(89)

Moreover, we have the following comparisons:
For
l\geq C_{0}+1
, we have

\|\ell_{Q}-\ell_{PQ}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{1})}\leq C(h_{Q}+h_{PQ});

(90)

whenever
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
and
TQ_{1}\cap TQ_{2}\neq\varnothing
, then

\|\ell_{Q_{1}}-\ell_{Q_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{1})}\leq C(h_{Q_{1}}+h_{Q_{2}}).

(91)

Finally, for
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{C_{0}}
we can take
\ell_{Q}(x^{\prime})=({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}
.

Proof.

It is convenient to assume—after a translation—that
{\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}=0
. Write
P=\left[\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{2}+W(u)\right]
. We divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1.
We first establish the following claim: Let

R\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]
. For
k
large enough, suppose that
\{\mathbf{z}_{j}\}_{j\in J}\subset\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}
is a collection with
\{B_{R}^{\prime}(\mathbf{z}_{j}^{\prime})\}_{j\in J}
pairwise disjoint; in particular, the unprojected balls
\{B_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\}_{j\in J}
are pairwise disjoint too. We claim then that

\sum_{j\in J}\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B^{\prime}_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j}^{\prime})\times\big(\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4},\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4}\big)}|x_{n}-\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})|^{2}P\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}+C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{n}e^{-cR},

(92)

where
\ell_{j}:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\to\mathbb{R}
are affine functions satisfying

R_{k}^{-1}|\ell_{j}(0)|+|\nabla\ell_{j}|\leq o_{k}(1).

(93)

Indeed,
Lemma

6.2
gives unit vectors
v_{j}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
such that

|v_{j}-e_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{j\in J}{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})\leq C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{1+\alpha}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2},

(94)

where

{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})=\frac{1}{(4R)^{n+1}}\int_{B_{4R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}|v_{j}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z}_{j})|^{2}P\,dx.

Using
Lemmas

4.5
and
6.1
, we have

|(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{n}|=|\mathbf{z}_{j}\cdot e_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1){\widetilde{R}_{k}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\{|u|\leq 0.9\}\subset\{x\ :\ |x_{n}-(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1)R\}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{in }B^{\prime}_{4R}(\mathbf{z}_{j}^{\prime})\times\big(\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{2},\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{2}\big)\,.

(95)

Set
a_{j}=\frac{v_{j}-(v_{j}\cdot e_{n})e_{n}}{v_{j}\cdot e_{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
and
b_{j}=\frac{v_{j}\cdot\mathbf{z}_{j}}{v_{j}\cdot e_{n}}\in\mathbb{R}
. Define
\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})=a_{j}\cdot x^{\prime}+b_{j}
, so that
{\rm graph}\,\ell_{j}=\{v_{j}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z}_{j})=0\}
.

Since
|\mathbf{z}_{j}\cdot e_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1){\widetilde{R}_{k}}
and
|v_{j}-e_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1)
, we get (
93
).

Moreover, notice that
|x_{n}-\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})|\leq 2|v_{j}\cdot(x-\mathbf{z}_{j})|
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
(for
k
large), thus we have:

\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B_{4R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}|x_{n}-\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})|^{2}P\,dx\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})\,.

We estimate the rest by exponential decay: by (
95
) and
Lemma

4.3
, we have

P\leq Ce^{-cR}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{in }B^{\prime}_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j}^{\prime})\times\big(\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4},\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4}\big)\setminus\{|x_{n}|\leq R\}\,,

and thus

\frac{1}{R^{n+1}}\int_{B^{\prime}_{R}(\mathbf{z}_{j})\times\big(\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4},\tfrac{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}{4}\big)}|x_{n}-\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})|^{2}P\leq C{\bf H}_{4R}^{2}(v_{j},\mathbf{z}_{j})+Ce^{-cR}.

Then (
92
) follows recalling (
94
) and using that at most
C({\widetilde{R}_{k}}/R)^{n}
disjoint balls of radius
R
fit inside the cylinder
B^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}_{k}}\times(-{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}})
—a brutal bound that is sufficient here because it is multiplied by the exponential.

Step 2.
We now combine the claim in Step 1 and a covering argument.

To each
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
, assign a point
\mathbf{z}_{Q}\in\mathcal{Z}
such that
Q\subset B^{\prime}_{60\,2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}(\mathbf{z}_{Q}^{\prime})
. Applying the Besicovitch covering lemma to the family of balls
\{B^{\prime}_{60\,2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}(\mathbf{z}_{Q}^{\prime})\}_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}}
, we obtain a dimensional constant
b_{n}
(with
n=4
) and a decomposition into
b_{n}
subfamilies, each consisting of pairwise disjoint balls, such that their union still covers the union of the cubes in
\mathcal{X}^{l}
. By Step 1 (with
R=2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
) applied to each of these subfamilies, (
87
) and (
89
) follow
21
21

21

After noticing that for
l\leq l_{\star}
we have
(2^{l})^{n}e^{-c2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\leq C(\varepsilon_{k})^{(\gamma-2)n}e^{-c\varepsilon_{k}^{-\gamma}}\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
for
k
large enough (since
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\geq 1
).
.

Finally, (
90
)-(
91
) can be obtained as straightforward consequences of (
54
)-(
55
) in
Lemma

4.7
.

Step 3.
Let us finally prove (
88
).

Let
\lambda=\lambda(n)>0
be such that, given any
x\in\{u=0\}
,
W(u)\geq\lambda
in
B_{\lambda}(x)
. Put
R=2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
. By Fubini,

\displaystyle h_{Q}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{R^{n+1}}\int_{Q\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}}\,dx^{\prime}\int_{g_{i}(x^{\prime})-\lambda}^{g_{i}(x^{\prime})+\lambda}\,dx_{n}|x_{n}-\ell_{Q}(x^{\prime})|^{2}P

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c\lambda}{R^{n+1}}\int_{Q\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}}\,dx^{\prime}\int_{g_{i}(x^{\prime})-\lambda}^{g_{i}(x^{\prime})+\lambda}\,dx_{n}|x_{n}-\ell_{Q}(x^{\prime})|^{2}\geq\frac{c}{R^{n+1}}\int_{Q\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}}\,dx^{\prime}|g_{i}(x^{\prime})-\ell_{j}(x^{\prime})|^{2}\,.

∎

We can now give the:

Proof of
Proposition

7.4
.

For
C_{0}\leq l\leq l_{\star}
, for each cube
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
let
\ell_{Q}:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be the affine function provided by Lemma

.
In particular,

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l}}(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{-2-(n-1)}\int_{\Omega_{2-\gamma/2}\cap TQ}|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}|^{2}\leq C2^{l(1+\alpha)}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for all }1\leq i\leq K_{*}.

(96)

We extend the definition of
\ell_{Q}
to the rest of cubes
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{l}
:
For
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{C_{0}}
we set

, and for

with

we define recursively
\ell_{Q}:=\ell_{PQ}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for }Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{l}

Approximating functions:
For
l\geq C_{0}
, define
22
22

22

Notice that, in particular, the domain of
\psi_{l}
contains the ball
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})
.

\psi_{l}:{\textstyle\bigcup\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\psi_{l}:=\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}}\eta_{Q}\ell_{Q}
.

Definition of
\widetilde{g}_{i}
.
Consider the cutoff function
\eta_{X}=\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}},\ Q\subset X}\eta_{Q}
,
which satisfies

\eta_{X}\equiv 1\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{in }X,\hskip 20.00003pt\eta_{X}\equiv 0\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{in }\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\setminus TX,\hskip 20.00003pt{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|\nabla\eta_{X}|+({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}|D^{2}\eta_{X}|\leq C.

We define
\widetilde{g}_{i}
by gluing
\psi_{l_{\star}}
and
g_{i}
via a cutoff:

\widetilde{g}_{i}:=\psi_{l_{\star}}\eta_{X}+g_{i}(1-\eta_{X})=\psi_{l_{\star}}+\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}}\eta_{Q}(1-\eta_{X})(g_{i}-\ell_{Q}).

Notice that (i) holds automatically by (
85
). To see (ii), observe first that it holds for
g_{i}
and over
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}
instead (directly by (
88
), taking

for
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{C_{0}}
). Now, by (
86
) we get the brutal bound
|\widetilde{g}_{i}(x^{\prime})-({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})_{n}|\leq o_{k}(1){\widetilde{R}_{k}}
in
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}
; using that this set is small easily gives (ii). By (
35
) and (
86
) we also get (iii).

Mean curvature estimate.
The main work is then in obtaining (iv).

We first focus on bounding
D^{2}\psi_{l_{\star}}
. By construction, for all
l\geq C_{0}+1
and
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
, we have

(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|_{Q}\equiv 0\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{whenever }TQ\cap{\textstyle\bigcup}(\mathcal{X}^{l})=\varnothing.

(97)

Then, combining (
89
) (applied at scales
l
and
l-1
) with (
90
)-(
91
) and using (
84
), we obtain
23
23

23

To see this, a useful observation is the following: if
TQ_{1}\cap(\bigcup\mathcal{X}^{l})\neq\varnothing
for some
Q_{1}\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
, and if
Q_{2}\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
is a contiguous cube such that
Q_{2}\subset TQ_{1}
, then the triples of their respective predecessors,
TPQ_{1}
and
TPQ_{2}
, must both belong to
\mathcal{X}^{l-1}
and necessarily intersect.

\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}}(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{-2}\sup_{Q}|\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1}|^{2}\leq C\,2^{l(1+\alpha)}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}.

(98)

Similarly:

\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}}(2^{-l}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|^{2}\leq C2^{l(1+\alpha)}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\,.

(99)

Then, Chebyshev’s inequality gives

\#\big\{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}\ :\ {\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|\geq t\big\}\leq\,\frac{1}{t^{2}}{2^{l(3+\alpha)}}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for all }t>0\,.

(100)

Next we use the above to show that for given
l
with
C_{0}\leq l\leq l_{*}
, setting
N_{l_{*}}:=\#\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}
we have

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|\leq CN_{l_{*}}^{1/2}2^{3l_{*}/2}2^{l\alpha/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}.

(101)

We distinguish two cases. Notice that
2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
is the number of cubes of
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}
that fit in a single cube of
\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
.

-
Case 1.
Suppose that
N_{l_{*}}\leq 2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
. Then, we bound the maximum by the sum in (
99
), obtaining
{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|\leq C2^{l(3+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}
for every
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
. Using this bound in the
Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}
and summing,

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|\leq CN_{l_{*}}2^{l(3+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\leq CN_{l_{*}}^{1/2}2^{3l_{*}/2}2^{l\alpha/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\,.

-
Case 2.
Suppose that
N_{l_{*}}\geq 2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
. The worse case scenario is then that the
N_{l_{*}}
“small” cubes in
\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}
keep filling the large cubes in
\boldsymbol{Q}^{l}
, starting from the ones contributing the most to the sum (
99
), and then continuing in decreasing order according to their contribution. Let
p^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
2^{p_{*}-1}\leq N_{l_{*}}/2^{3(l_{*}-l)}\leq 2^{p_{*}}
. We then use (
100
) with

\frac{1}{t^{2}}{2^{l(3+\alpha)}}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}=2^{p-1}
to estimate the maximal possible contribution of the
2^{p-1}2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
cubes that occupy positions
2^{p-1}2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
to
2^{p}2^{3(l_{*}-l)}
in (
101
). Doing so we obtain:

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{*}}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}(\psi_{l}-\psi_{l-1})|\leq C\sum^{p_{*}}_{p=0}2^{3(l_{*}-l)}2^{p}\frac{{2^{l(3+\alpha)/2}}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}}{2^{p/2}}\leq C2^{3(l_{*}-l)}2^{p_{*}/2}2^{l(3+\alpha)/2}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\leq CN_{l_{*}}^{1/2}2^{3l_{*}/2}2^{l\alpha/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}.

Combining the two cases gives (
101
). Summing (
101
) for
C_{0}\leq l\leq l_{\star}
, and using that
\psi_{C_{0}}\equiv 0
, we reach:

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{\star}}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sup_{Q}|D^{2}\psi_{l_{\star}}|\leq CN_{l_{*}}^{1/2}2^{l_{*}(3+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\,.

We now transform this into an integral bound.
Note that cubes
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}
have volume
(2^{-l_{\star}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}\leq(2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}
and cover
B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})
. Moreover,
2^{l_{\star}}\leq{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-(2-\gamma)}
and
N_{l_{*}}\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}
. We then obtain

\begin{split}\int_{\bigcup\mathcal{X}^{l}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|D^{2}\psi_{l_{\star}}|&\leq\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{X}^{l_{\star}}}(2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}\sup_{Q}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|D^{2}\psi_{l_{\star}}|\leq CN_{l_{*}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-(2-\gamma)(3+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}\\
&\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+(2-\gamma)(3-\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{3}\,.\end{split}

(102)

Notice that
\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]=L_{i}\widetilde{g}_{i}:={\rm tr}(A_{i}(x^{\prime})D^{2}\widetilde{g}_{i})
,
for some
|A_{i}(x^{\prime})-{\rm Id}|\leq o_{k}(1)
by (iii). We split our desired estimate into regions.

Region 1:
Since
\eta_{X}\equiv 1
in
X
, by (
102
) we reach

\int_{X\cap B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{\prime}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|=\int_{B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}}|L_{i}\psi_{l_{\star}}|\leq C\int_{B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{\prime}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}}|D^{2}\psi_{l_{\star}}|\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+(2-\gamma)(3-\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}.

The remaining estimates will involve
g_{i}
as well.

Region 2:
Since
\eta_{X}\equiv 0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\setminus TX
and all points in this domain are at distance
\geq 2^{-4}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
from
[\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})]^{\prime}
, using (
16
) and then (
80
) we obtain

\int_{(B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{\prime}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma})\setminus TX}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|=\int_{(B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{\prime}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma})\setminus TX}\big|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]\big|\leq C\frac{|B^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{\prime}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})\setminus\Omega_{2-\gamma}|}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}}\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})\,.

Region 3:
We conclude by bounding
\int_{TX\setminus X}|L_{i}(\widetilde{g}_{i}-\psi_{l_{\star}})|
. Observe that (
96
) implies that
24
24

24

To go from the first inequality to the second one, we use that the number of cubes is bounded by
CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}
, the inequality between the arithmetic and quadratic means, and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality.

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{*}}\\
Q\subset TX\setminus X\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(2^{-l_{\star}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}}\fint_{TQ}|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}|^{2}\leq C2^{l_{\star}(1+\alpha)}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{thus}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{*}}\\
Q\subset TX\setminus X\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\fint_{TQ}|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}|\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}2^{l_{\star}(1+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}\,.

Note:
Similar estimates hold replacing
\ell_{Q}
by
\ell_{Q_{1}}
, where
Q_{1}
is any adjacent cube with
Q_{1}\subset TQ
, by (
91
).

Since
g_{i}
is almost a minimal graph, verifying

\|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]\|_{C^{\theta}}\leq C({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{-2}
by (
16
), standard elliptic estimates give

\sup_{Q}\bigg(\frac{|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}|}{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}+|\nabla(g_{i}-\ell_{Q})|+{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|D^{2}(g_{i}-\ell_{Q})|\bigg)\leq C\fint_{TQ}\frac{|g_{i}-\ell_{Q}|}{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}+\frac{C}{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\,,

for every cube
Q
in
\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}
, with
Q\subset TX\setminus X
.

Using these estimates, noticing that in
TX\setminus X
we have
\widetilde{g}_{i}-\psi_{l_{\star}}=\sum_{Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}}\eta_{Q}(1-\eta_{X})(g_{i}-\ell_{Q})
,
and using the standard product rules to compute the second derivatives of products we obtain:

\sum_{Q\subset TX\setminus X}\sup_{Q}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}|L_{i}(\widetilde{g}_{i}-\psi_{l_{\star}})|\leq CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}2^{l_{\star}(1+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}+\frac{C}{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\#\{Q\subset TX\setminus X\}.

Finally, since the volume of each cube
Q\in\boldsymbol{Q}^{l_{\star}}
is
(2^{-l_{\star}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-1}
, with
n=4
, and
2^{-\ell_{*}}
is comparable to
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}
we obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{TX\setminus X}|L_{i}(\widetilde{g}_{i}-\psi_{l_{\star}})|&\leq\frac{(C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{n-1}}{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}\bigg(N_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}2^{l_{\star}(1+\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}+\frac{\#\{Q\subset TX\setminus X\}}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\bigg)\\
&\leq C{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}N_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+(2-\gamma)(3-\alpha)/2}+CN_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}.\end{split}

Adding all the previous estimates completes the proof.
∎

7.2
Linearised equation

Recall that
\gamma=\frac{1}{4}
. We show that our extended graphs are very harmonic in an
L^{1}
sense.

Proposition 7.6

(
Linearised equation).

Fix
\chi\in(0,\frac{1}{20}]
,
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{40}]
and
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{40}]
. For every
\bar{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{8}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}))^{\prime}
we have that

\frac{1}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\rho^{2}\fint_{B_{\rho}^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\frac{1}{10}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+2\chi}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},\frac{1}{8}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]\,,

(103)

provided that
k
is large enough.

Remark 7.7
.

This should be interpreted as follows: up to scale
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+2\chi}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
, the deviation of the
\widetilde{g}_{i}
from an approximating minimal graph is small
25
25

25

In fact, it is of size
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+\frac{1}{10}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
, thus smaller than the size of the
\widetilde{g}_{i}
by rate of a small power
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\frac{1}{10}}
.
relative to the size of the
\widetilde{g}_{i}
themselves, which is
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
. A key point is that our analysis penetrates the “critical scale”
{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
, as explained below in the proof.

Proof.

We will show that

\int_{B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-2\beta}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}

(104)

and moreover

\int_{B_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbf{z}})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2+\frac{1}{5}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pt\bar{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{Z}\cap B_{\frac{1}{8}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}))^{\prime}\,.

(105)

Combining them immediately implies (
103
): Indeed, considering (
104
) and the fact that
\beta\leq\frac{1}{8}
, we immediately see that

\frac{1}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\rho^{2}\fint_{B_{\rho}^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbf{z}})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1-\frac{1}{4}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\rho^{-1}\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]\,.

Likewise,multiplying (
105
) by
\rho^{-1}
and using
\chi\leq\frac{1}{20}
we find that

\frac{1}{({\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}})}\rho^{2}\fint_{B_{\rho}^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbf{z}})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+\frac{1}{5}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\rho^{-1}\leq C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{\frac{1}{10}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{for every}\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in[{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+2\chi}{\widetilde{R}_{k}},{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}]\,.

It remains to establish (
104
)-(
105
). We divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1.
Estimating
\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]
.

The second term in (
81
) is small: By (
31
),
\gamma=\frac{1}{4}
, and the bounds for
\alpha
and
\beta
, we have

\sqrt{N_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}}\,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+(2-\gamma)(3-\alpha)/2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\leq{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{-\frac{7}{8}(1+\frac{1}{40})}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+\frac{7}{8}(3-\frac{1}{40})}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}={\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{1+\frac{7}{8}(2-\frac{2}{40})}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\leq{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2+\frac{1}{5}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\,.

(106)

Given
V\subset B_{\frac{1}{4}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
, (
81
) then gives

\displaystyle\int_{V}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{V\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}}|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]|+C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}N_{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}}+C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2+\frac{1}{5}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\,.

By
Theorem

2.14
we know that
|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]|\leq Cd^{-2}
, so that given
\theta\in(0,1/2]
, together with (
80
) we can bound

\displaystyle\int_{\{\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\leq d\leq 2\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})}|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]|\leq C\frac{(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{3}N_{\theta}}{(\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}})^{2}}=C\theta{\widetilde{R}_{k}}N_{\theta}\,.

Denoting
\theta_{j}=2^{-j}
, and letting
1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}
with
\theta_{j_{2}}\leq{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}\leq\theta_{j_{2}-1}
, by summing we find that

\int_{\{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\leq d\leq\theta_{j_{1}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})}|\operatorname{H}[g_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sum_{j_{1}\leq j\leq j_{2}}\theta_{j}N_{\theta_{j}}\,,

or (letting
V=\{d\leq\theta_{j_{1}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
, so that
V\cap\Omega_{2-\gamma}=\{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2-\gamma}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\leq d\leq\theta_{j_{1}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})
)

\int_{\{d\leq\theta_{j_{1}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\}\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{\prime}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}}^{\prime})}|\operatorname{H}[\widetilde{g}_{i}]|\leq C{\widetilde{R}_{k}}\sum_{j_{1}\leq j\leq j_{2}}\theta_{j}N_{\theta_{j}}+C{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2+\frac{1}{5}}{\widetilde{R}_{k}}^{2}\,.

(107)

Step 2.
Proof of (
104
).

We will use the following bounds:

•

N_{\theta}\leq\theta^{-1-\beta}
. This follows from (
31
).

•

c\leq{\widetilde{R}_{k}}{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
. This follows from (
60
) with
R=4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}
combined with
{\bf H}_{4{\widetilde{R}_{k}}}^{2}({\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}})\leq 2{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{2}
(see
Lemma

3.18
).

Choosing
j_{1}=1
in (
107
),
